Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say consistently, no. I’ve had off days, definitely, but most of the day nearly every day for three months sounds… stronger than what I’d want to say. I don’t think that’s been the case for me. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even small things, like unloading the dishwasher or trying to read for my certificate class, can feel like… a lot. I know that probably sounds kind of minor, but it’s been hard to keep up.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Maybe a little down here and there, but not like… crying all the time or anything. It’s more that I’m just worn out, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, I guess maybe less than usual. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that normally make me feel like me—like sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga… even tennis with a friend. I still could do them, probably, I just… don’t really feel pulled toward them lately. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: Um… anxious? I’m not totally sure. I wouldn’t say I’m on edge all the time or anything like that. Maybe I get a little tense when I’m not keeping up with things, but… I don’t know if that counts as anxiety. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. I mean, I’m not trying to lose weight or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off. Some days I’ll realize I’ve basically just had coffee and a few bites of something, which… I know isn’t great. And my jeans have been fitting looser. I don’t know if it’s enough to matter, but I have noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I feel kind of tired of everything, but not like I don’t want to be alive. I’m okay saying that, I think.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been around three months. Not awful every single day, but this quieter low stretch has kind of been there, with me feeling less interested in things and really tired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I probably overthink things I’ve said or done, like anyone does, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling guilty in a big way. At least, nothing that’s been standing out to me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t think it’s dramatic or anything, but on my slower days it honestly feels like my body is moving through wet cement. And sometimes even answering a simple question from my spouse takes me… weirdly long. I know that probably sounds silly, but it does happen.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're worthless or a failure lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a serious way. I mean, I have moments where I’m kind of hard on myself or feel like I’m not doing enough, but I wouldn’t say I feel worthless. That sounds really extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say consistently, no. I’ve had off days, definitely, but most of the day nearly every day for three months sounds… stronger than what I’d want to say. I don’t think that’s been the case for me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I wouldn’t say I can’t enjoy anything at all, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga… even playing tennis with a friend just feels like something I should want to do, not something I actually feel excited about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood or feeling down is present for more than half of the days over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say more than half the days, no. I mean, I have had days where I feel kind of low or flat, but when you put it that way—over three months, more than half the days—that feels like maybe too much. I haven’t really been tracking it exactly, though. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy, even if you're not necessarily motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I can still sometimes enjoy things once I’m doing them, I think… but it’s definitely dulled. Like, I’ve stopped caring as much about the things that usually make me feel like myself—sketching, gallery openings, cooking, yoga, even tennis with a friend. I’ll do them sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than really taking pleasure in them. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling depressed most of the time over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sort of. I’m hesitant to say “depressed most of the time” because that sounds so dramatic, but there’s been this quieter low stretch for about three months now. Mostly it’s like I don’t have much interest in things, and I feel really heavy and tired. Does that count as depressed most of the time?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I wouldn’t say I can’t enjoy anything at all, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga… even playing tennis with a friend just feels like something I should want to do, not something I actually feel excited about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood or feeling down is present for more than half of the days over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say more than half the days, no. I mean, I have had days where I feel kind of low or flat, but when you put it that way—over three months, more than half the days—that feels like maybe too much. I haven’t really been tracking it exactly, though. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy, even if you're not necessarily motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I can still sometimes enjoy things once I’m doing them, I think… but it’s definitely dulled. Like, I’ve stopped caring as much about the things that usually make me feel like myself—sketching, gallery openings, cooking, yoga, even tennis with a friend. I’ll do them sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than really taking pleasure in them. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling depressed most of the time over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sort of. Mostly it’s like I don’t have much interest in things, and I feel really heavy and tired. Does that count as depressed most of the time?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of tiredness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is, unfortunately. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ve fallen behind in my certificate coursework, and little things like bills, meals, and house stuff have been piling up because I can’t seem to keep my plans straight or follow through consistently.\n\nMy spouse has had to pick up more at home, which I feel bad about. And I’ve kind of pulled back from friends and some art commitments I normally really care about. So… yes, I guess it is affecting things. Is that the kind of interference you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think “significantly” is probably fair, even if I don’t love admitting that. I mean… I probably overthink things I’ve said or done, like anyone does, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling guilty in a big way. At least, nothing that’s been standing out to me. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t think it’s dramatic or anything, but on my slower days it honestly feels like my body is moving through wet cement. And sometimes even answering a simple question from my spouse takes me… weirdly long. I know that probably sounds silly, but it does happen.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're worthless or a failure lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a serious way. I mean, I have moments where I’m kind of hard on myself or feel like I’m not doing enough, but I wouldn’t say I feel worthless. That sounds really extreme, and I don’t want to overstate it. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say consistently, no. I’ve had off days, definitely, but most of the day nearly every day for three months sounds… stronger than what I’d want to say. I don’t think that’s been the case for me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I wouldn’t say I can’t enjoy anything at all, but I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga… even playing tennis with a friend just feels like something I should want to do, not something I actually feel excited about. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood or feeling down is present for more than half of the days over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say more than half the days, no. I mean, I have had days where I feel kind of low or flat, but when you put it that way—over three months, more than half the days—that feels like maybe too much. I haven’t really been tracking it exactly, though. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking pleasure in activities that you would normally enjoy, even if you're not necessarily motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: I can still sometimes enjoy things once I’m doing them, I think… but it’s definitely dulled. Like, I’ve stopped caring as much about the things that usually make me feel like myself—sketching, gallery openings, cooking, yoga, even tennis with a friend. I’ll do them sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than really taking pleasure in them. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you've been feeling depressed most of the time over the past three months?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, sort of. I’m hesitant to say “depressed most of the time” because that sounds so dramatic, but there’s been this quieter low stretch for about three months now. Mostly it’s like I don’t have much interest in things, and I feel really heavy and tired. Does that count as depressed most of the time?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of tiredness and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is, unfortunately. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’ve fallen behind in my certificate coursework, and little things like bills, meals, and house stuff have been piling up because I can’t seem to keep my plans straight or follow through consistently.\n\nMy spouse has had to pick up more at home, which I feel bad about. And I’ve kind of pulled back from friends and some art commitments I normally really care about. So… yes, I guess it is affecting things. Is that the kind of interference you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is significantly impacting your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think “significantly” is probably fair, even if I don’t love admitting that. It’s not like I’m doing nothing at all, but I’ve fallen behind in my certificate coursework, and regular life stuff—bills, meals, household tasks—has been piling up because I can’t seem to keep things organized or follow through.\n\nMy spouse has had to step in more at home, which I feel really guilty about. And I’ve pulled back from friends and art commitments I normally care about. So… yes, it is affecting my ability to function day to day. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low mood is present nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to judge “nearly every day.” It feels like it’s been around a lot, kind of in the background most days, but I don’t want to overstate it if there are moments where I seem okay or get distracted.\n\nSo… maybe yes, but not always intense? Does that answer it the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has your low mood lasted for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has been more than two weeks. I was kind of hoping it was just a short rough patch, but this quieter low stretch—with not much interest in things and feeling really tired—has been going on for about three months now.\n\nIt’s not dramatic every single day, so I don’t want to overstate it, but… yes, it’s definitely been longer than two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your low mood as being consistently present, even if mild, for most of the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it. It’s been kind of consistently there, even if it’s not always intense or obvious from the outside.\n\nThe low interest and that heavy tiredness have been going on for about three months now. I keep wanting to say it’s “just a phase” or not that bad, but… most of the time, it’s been there in some form.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
